I still do them, just… I don’t look forward to them as much, if that’s an okay way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least somewhat. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do those things—I still go to the lab, I’ll bake sometimes, I check the telescope—but most days there’s this low, gray mood kind of running underneath everything. So the things I used to enjoy don’t feel as light or exciting anymore. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not really significant, no. I mean, there are better days here and there, and I don’t want to make it sound like it’s been exactly the same every single day, but the low baseline has been there pretty steadily since shortly after we moved into that older house… a little over two years ago. Is that what you mean by improvement?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in almost all activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Almost all… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. I can still do things, like go to the lab and talk to people, or bake, or check the telescope. But most days there’s this low, gray feeling in the background, and those things don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. So… not completely gone, but definitely dulled, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks in the past two years where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience that low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days that were better, and I probably looked pretty normal from the outside, but that low, gray baseline has kind of been there since shortly after we moved into the older house, a little over two years ago. I don’t think there’s been a full two weeks where it was just… gone. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t weigh myself very often, so I guess I can’t say perfectly, but I don’t think there’s been any big change. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “extremely” sad, exactly… I don’t want to overstate it. But most days, yes, there’s this low, gray mood sort of running in the background for a lot of the day. I can still go to the lab and talk to people and do things, but I don’t feel light or excited about things like baking or checking the telescope the way I used to. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. I still see people when I need to, and I don’t avoid everyone or anything like that. Maybe I’m a little less eager sometimes, but… it doesn’t feel like a dramatic change to me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes… though I don’t know if I’d say I no longer enjoy them at all. It’s more like most days there’s this low, gray mood in the background, and the things I used to look forward to—like baking, or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. I can still do them, and I still function at the lab and talk to people, but the pleasure feels… muted, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt so depressed that it was very difficult for you to carry out daily activities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not so depressed that I couldn’t function, exactly. I’m still getting my lab work done, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But things do take longer than they should—grant drafts, routine project decisions, that kind of thing. And at home I’ve kind of let go of the baking, the bonsai, the telescope plans… the things that used to give my downtime some structure. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying the things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, I think so. I mean, I still do things—I go to the lab, I talk to people, I can function—but most days there’s just this kind of low, gray feeling in the background. Things like baking, or checking the telescope, they don’t really feel… light or exciting the way they used to. It’s not like I can’t do them at all, but I don’t get much enjoyment out of them anymore, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: I guess… if I’m being honest, it’s been a little over two years. It started not long after we moved into the older house. I kept thinking it was just the stress of settling in, and it’s not like I’ve been unable to manage, but that low background feeling has kind of stayed there since then. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: A little, I think… I don’t want to make it sound like nothing feels good at all. I still bake sometimes, and I still check the telescope, and I can talk with people at the lab. But most days there’s this low, gray sort of mood in the background, so I don’t really feel light or excited about those things the way I used to. I still do them, just… I don’t look forward to them as much, if that’s an okay way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least somewhat. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do those things—I still go to the lab, I’ll bake sometimes, I check the telescope—but most days there’s this low, gray mood kind of running underneath everything. So the things I used to enjoy don’t feel as light or exciting anymore. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not really significant, no. I mean, there are better days here and there, and I don’t want to make it sound like it’s been exactly the same every single day, but the low baseline has been there pretty steadily since shortly after we moved into that older house… a little over two years ago. Is that what you mean by improvement?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in almost all activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Almost all… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. I can still do things, like go to the lab and talk to people, or bake, or check the telescope. But most days there’s this low, gray feeling in the background, and those things don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. So… not completely gone, but definitely dulled, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks in the past two years where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience that low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days that were better, and I probably looked pretty normal from the outside, but that low, gray baseline has kind of been there since shortly after we moved into the older house, a little over two years ago. I don’t think there’s been a full two weeks where it was just… gone. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t weigh myself very often, so I guess I can’t say perfectly, but I don’t think there’s been any big change. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “extremely” sad, exactly… I don’t want to overstate it. But most days, yes, there’s this low, gray mood sort of running in the background for a lot of the day. I can still go to the lab and talk to people and do things, but I don’t feel light or excited about things like baking or checking the telescope the way I used to. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. I still see people when I need to, and I don’t avoid everyone or anything like that. Maybe I’m a little less eager sometimes, but… it doesn’t feel like a dramatic change to me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes… though I don’t know if I’d say I no longer enjoy them at all. It’s more like most days there’s this low, gray mood in the background, and the things I used to look forward to—like baking, or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. I can still do them, and I still function at the lab and talk to people, but the pleasure feels… muted, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt so depressed that it was very difficult for you to carry out daily activities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not so depressed that I couldn’t function, exactly. I’m still getting my lab work done, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But things do take longer than they should—grant drafts, routine project decisions, that kind of thing. And at home I’ve kind of let go of the baking, the bonsai, the telescope plans… the things that used to give my downtime some structure. So, yes, maybe it has made daily life harder, just not in a very dramatic way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so depressed that you couldn't get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, not really in the sense of being unable to get out of bed. I do get up and I’m still getting my lab work done. It’s more that things feel slower than they should—grant drafts take me longer, and even routine project decisions can drag. And at home I’ve let the baking, bonsai, and telescope plans kind of fall away. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been stuck in bed, if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I feel nothing at all. It’s more like there’s this low, gray feeling in the background most days, and things I used to feel genuinely excited about—like baking or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as enjoyable or light as they used to. I can still do them, or at least I could, but the pleasure is pretty muted. Does that answer what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I still do them, just… I don’t look forward to them as much, if that’s an okay way to put it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, at least somewhat. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do those things—I still go to the lab, I’ll bake sometimes, I check the telescope—but most days there’s this low, gray mood kind of running underneath everything. So the things I used to enjoy don’t feel as light or exciting anymore. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not really significant, no. I mean, there are better days here and there, and I don’t want to make it sound like it’s been exactly the same every single day, but the low baseline has been there pretty steadily since shortly after we moved into that older house… a little over two years ago. Is that what you mean by improvement?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in almost all activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Almost all… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. I can still do things, like go to the lab and talk to people, or bake, or check the telescope. But most days there’s this low, gray feeling in the background, and those things don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. So… not completely gone, but definitely dulled, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks in the past two years where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience that low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days that were better, and I probably looked pretty normal from the outside, but that low, gray baseline has kind of been there since shortly after we moved into the older house, a little over two years ago. I don’t think there’s been a full two weeks where it was just… gone. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t weigh myself very often, so I guess I can’t say perfectly, but I don’t think there’s been any big change. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “extremely” sad, exactly… I don’t want to overstate it. But most days, yes, there’s this low, gray mood sort of running in the background for a lot of the day. I can still go to the lab and talk to people and do things, but I don’t feel light or excited about things like baking or checking the telescope the way I used to. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. I still see people when I need to, and I don’t avoid everyone or anything like that. Maybe I’m a little less eager sometimes, but… it doesn’t feel like a dramatic change to me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes… though I don’t know if I’d say I no longer enjoy them at all. It’s more like most days there’s this low, gray mood in the background, and the things I used to look forward to—like baking, or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. I can still do them, and I still function at the lab and talk to people, but the pleasure feels… muted, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt so depressed that it was very difficult for you to carry out daily activities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not so depressed that I couldn’t function, exactly. I’m still getting my lab work done, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But things do take longer than they should—grant drafts, routine project decisions, that kind of thing. And at home I’ve kind of let go of the baking, the bonsai, the telescope plans… the things that used to give my downtime some structure. So, yes, maybe it has made daily life harder, just not in a very dramatic way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so depressed that you couldn't get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, not really in the sense of being unable to get out of bed. I do get up and I’m still getting my lab work done. It’s more that things feel slower than they should—grant drafts take me longer, and even routine project decisions can drag. And at home I’ve let the baking, bonsai, and telescope plans kind of fall away. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been stuck in bed, if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I feel nothing at all. It’s more like there’s this low, gray feeling in the background most days, and things I used to feel genuinely excited about—like baking or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as enjoyable or light as they used to. I can still do them, or at least I could, but the pleasure is pretty muted. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two months where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience any of the low, gray feelings you've described?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve had better stretches, but not a full couple of months where I felt completely back to normal. This kind of low baseline has been there since shortly after we moved into the older house… a little over two years ago now. It’s not dramatic every day, but it hasn’t really gone away for that long. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

But most days there’s this low, gray feeling in the background, and those things don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. So… not completely gone, but definitely dulled, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks in the past two years where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience that low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days that were better, and I probably looked pretty normal from the outside, but that low, gray baseline has kind of been there since shortly after we moved into the older house, a little over two years ago. I don’t think there’s been a full two weeks where it was just… gone. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t weigh myself very often, so I guess I can’t say perfectly, but I don’t think there’s been any big change. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “extremely” sad, exactly… I don’t want to overstate it. But most days, yes, there’s this low, gray mood sort of running in the background for a lot of the day. I can still go to the lab and talk to people and do things, but I don’t feel light or excited about things like baking or checking the telescope the way I used to. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. I still see people when I need to, and I don’t avoid everyone or anything like that. Maybe I’m a little less eager sometimes, but… it doesn’t feel like a dramatic change to me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes… though I don’t know if I’d say I no longer enjoy them at all. It’s more like most days there’s this low, gray mood in the background, and the things I used to look forward to—like baking, or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. I can still do them, and I still function at the lab and talk to people, but the pleasure feels… muted, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt so depressed that it was very difficult for you to carry out daily activities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not so depressed that I couldn’t function, exactly. I’m still getting my lab work done, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But things do take longer than they should—grant drafts, routine project decisions, that kind of thing. And at home I’ve kind of let go of the baking, the bonsai, the telescope plans… the things that used to give my downtime some structure. So, yes, maybe it has made daily life harder, just not in a very dramatic way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so depressed that you couldn't get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, not really in the sense of being unable to get out of bed. I do get up and I’m still getting my lab work done. It’s more that things feel slower than they should—grant drafts take me longer, and even routine project decisions can drag. And at home I’ve let the baking, bonsai, and telescope plans kind of fall away. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been stuck in bed, if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I feel nothing at all. It’s more like there’s this low, gray feeling in the background most days, and things I used to feel genuinely excited about—like baking or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as enjoyable or light as they used to. I can still do them, or at least I could, but the pleasure is pretty muted. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two months where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience any of the low, gray feelings you've described?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve had better stretches, but not a full couple of months where I felt completely back to normal. This kind of low baseline has been there since shortly after we moved into the older house… a little over two years ago now. It’s not dramatic every day, but it hasn’t really gone away for that long. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation during this period of low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt worn down and discouraged, but I haven’t had thoughts about wanting to die or anything like that. At least not in a way I’d call suicidal. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I can still do things, like go to the lab and talk to people, or bake, or check the telescope. But most days there’s this low, gray feeling in the background, and those things don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. So… not completely gone, but definitely dulled, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks in the past two years where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience that low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days that were better, and I probably looked pretty normal from the outside, but that low, gray baseline has kind of been there since shortly after we moved into the older house, a little over two years ago. I don’t think there’s been a full two weeks where it was just… gone. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t weigh myself very often, so I guess I can’t say perfectly, but I don’t think there’s been any big change. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “extremely” sad, exactly… I don’t want to overstate it. But most days, yes, there’s this low, gray mood sort of running in the background for a lot of the day. I can still go to the lab and talk to people and do things, but I don’t feel light or excited about things like baking or checking the telescope the way I used to. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. I still see people when I need to, and I don’t avoid everyone or anything like that. Maybe I’m a little less eager sometimes, but… it doesn’t feel like a dramatic change to me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes… though I don’t know if I’d say I no longer enjoy them at all. It’s more like most days there’s this low, gray mood in the background, and the things I used to look forward to—like baking, or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. I can still do them, and I still function at the lab and talk to people, but the pleasure feels… muted, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt so depressed that it was very difficult for you to carry out daily activities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not so depressed that I couldn’t function, exactly. I’m still getting my lab work done, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But things do take longer than they should—grant drafts, routine project decisions, that kind of thing. And at home I’ve kind of let go of the baking, the bonsai, the telescope plans… the things that used to give my downtime some structure. So, yes, maybe it has made daily life harder, just not in a very dramatic way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so depressed that you couldn't get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, not really in the sense of being unable to get out of bed. I do get up and I’m still getting my lab work done. It’s more that things feel slower than they should—grant drafts take me longer, and even routine project decisions can drag. And at home I’ve let the baking, bonsai, and telescope plans kind of fall away. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been stuck in bed, if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I feel nothing at all. It’s more like there’s this low, gray feeling in the background most days, and things I used to feel genuinely excited about—like baking or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as enjoyable or light as they used to. I can still do them, or at least I could, but the pleasure is pretty muted. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two months where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience any of the low, gray feelings you've described?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve had better stretches, but not a full couple of months where I felt completely back to normal. This kind of low baseline has been there since shortly after we moved into the older house… a little over two years ago now. It’s not dramatic every day, but it hasn’t really gone away for that long. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation during this period of low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt worn down and discouraged, but I haven’t had thoughts about wanting to die or anything like that. At least not in a way I’d call suicidal. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but… not really as much as I used to, no. I can still do things, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just miserable all the time. I still go to the lab, I can talk to people, I can get through the day. But most days there’s this low, gray feeling in the background, and things like baking or checking the telescope don’t feel light or exciting the way they used to. The enjoyment is there sometimes, just… kind of dulled. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still do those things—I still go to the lab, I’ll bake sometimes, I check the telescope—but most days there’s this low, gray mood kind of running underneath everything. So the things I used to enjoy don’t feel as light or exciting anymore. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of significant improvement in your mood over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not really significant, no. I mean, there are better days here and there, and I don’t want to make it sound like it’s been exactly the same every single day, but the low baseline has been there pretty steadily since shortly after we moved into that older house… a little over two years ago. Is that what you mean by improvement?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost pleasure in almost all activities most of the time?\n\nPatient: Almost all… I don’t know if I’d say that exactly. I can still do things, like go to the lab and talk to people, or bake, or check the telescope. But most days there’s this low, gray feeling in the background, and those things don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. So… not completely gone, but definitely dulled, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks in the past two years where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience that low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had days that were better, and I probably looked pretty normal from the outside, but that low, gray baseline has kind of been there since shortly after we moved into the older house, a little over two years ago. I don’t think there’s been a full two weeks where it was just… gone. Is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. My clothes seem to fit about the same. I don’t weigh myself very often, so I guess I can’t say perfectly, but I don’t think there’s been any big change. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt extremely sad or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say “extremely” sad, exactly… I don’t want to overstate it. But most days, yes, there’s this low, gray mood sort of running in the background for a lot of the day. I can still go to the lab and talk to people and do things, but I don’t feel light or excited about things like baking or checking the telescope the way I used to. Does that count as what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest exactly. I still see people when I need to, and I don’t avoid everyone or anything like that. Maybe I’m a little less eager sometimes, but… it doesn’t feel like a dramatic change to me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes… though I don’t know if I’d say I no longer enjoy them at all. It’s more like most days there’s this low, gray mood in the background, and the things I used to look forward to—like baking, or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as light or exciting as they used to. I can still do them, and I still function at the lab and talk to people, but the pleasure feels… muted, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you felt so depressed that it was very difficult for you to carry out daily activities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not so depressed that I couldn’t function, exactly. I’m still getting my lab work done, so I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But things do take longer than they should—grant drafts, routine project decisions, that kind of thing. And at home I’ve kind of let go of the baking, the bonsai, the telescope plans… the things that used to give my downtime some structure. So, yes, maybe it has made daily life harder, just not in a very dramatic way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so depressed that you couldn't get out of bed?\n\nPatient: No, not really in the sense of being unable to get out of bed. I do get up and I’m still getting my lab work done. It’s more that things feel slower than they should—grant drafts take me longer, and even routine project decisions can drag. And at home I’ve let the baking, bonsai, and telescope plans kind of fall away. But I wouldn’t say I’ve been stuck in bed, if that answers it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you no longer find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time, yes… though I don’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I feel nothing at all. It’s more like there’s this low, gray feeling in the background most days, and things I used to feel genuinely excited about—like baking or checking on the telescope—just don’t feel as enjoyable or light as they used to. I can still do them, or at least I could, but the pleasure is pretty muted. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least two months where you felt completely back to normal and didn't experience any of the low, gray feelings you've described?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’ve had better stretches, but not a full couple of months where I felt completely back to normal. This kind of low baseline has been there since shortly after we moved into the older house… a little over two years ago now. It’s not dramatic every day, but it hasn’t really gone away for that long. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideation during this period of low, gray feeling?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt worn down and discouraged, but I haven’t had thoughts about wanting to die or anything like that. At least not in a way I’d call suicidal. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there are any activities that you still enjoy and look forward to as much as you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but… not really as much as I used to, no. I can still do things, and I don’t want to make it sound like I’m just miserable all the time. I still go to the lab, I can talk to people, I can get through the day. But most days there’s this low, gray feeling in the background, and things like baking or checking the telescope don’t feel light or exciting the way they used to. The enjoyment is there sometimes, just… kind of dulled. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Has your low, gray feeling lasted for two years continuously or have there been breaks?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty continuous, I think. There have been better days or stretches where I manage fine, but not a real break from it. It started shortly after we moved into the older house, and that was a little over two years ago now. I hope I’m explaining that clearly.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
